<div class="issue-lead">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">01 &middot; Culture</h2>
<h1 class="issue-story-heading issue-lead-headline">Just Be For Real: On <em class="issue-emphasis">Impersonators</em>, Scammers &amp; Stolen Identities!</h1>
<div class="issue-lead-body">The author&#39;s Substack profile was impersonated by a scammer (@locarmenn) who followed subscribers attempting to deceive people Musicians&#39; identities are increasingly being stolen for romance scams, catfishing, and fraudulent impersonation on social media platforms Historical and contemporary examples of identity theft include fake Bruno Mars profiles that scammed a woman out of $100k, fake guitar websites, and AI-generated music theft targeting indie artists The 1968 case of fake Zombies bands created by a Michigan booking agent is explored, including how two members went on to form ZZ Top AI identity theft scams are now targeting the indie music community, with artists unable to prove ownership or remove false copyright claims</div>
<div class="issue-lead-cta">
<a class="issue-cta-btn" href="/2026/08/16/stories/just-be-for-real-on-impersonators-scammers-stolen-identities-0550f5/">Read full piece →</a>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://links.tldrnewsletter.com/G9OP1T">Apple Explores Shaking Up Its Smartwatch for a New Era</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="apple-explores-shaking-up-its-smartwatch-for-a-new-era-d3a27a"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">The Apple Watch continues to attract new buyers and remains the top-selling smartwatch. However, the company&#39;s industrial design team has been exploring a broad rethink of Apple&#39;s smartwatches and is evaluating several different directions. Apple needs to adapt to the rise of screenless fitness bands and smart rings, which are cheaper, lighter, and more fun and convenient to use for many people. The company aims to inject new life into its Wearables, Home, and Accessories segment, which has struggled to generate meaningful growth in recent years.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=f6b93b62-9498-11f1-b407-617d207c3f6a%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786356070%26t=1786359902%26s=74af2209c230a330bc89fc8a8548c43ac279017a75ea47abb8646f99d405931b/1/0100019feb58f02b-b9cd628c-a490-43d3-8cb6-db5f7e5b0340-000000/psYd67HjVJrQUzip8J0z9-_IyH9_mJ4GMRMtx76jGhI=452">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">OpenAI recently gave a presentation about the Hugging Face incident. The talk is short and information dense and provides full details of what happened and how things played out inside OpenAI. This post presents the timeline of events and details how the hack occurred. A video of the presentation is available in the post.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://links.tldrnewsletter.com/ctTFv3">China-Free Batteries Made From Salt Are Finally Here</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="china-free-batteries-made-from-salt-are-finally-here-d3df37"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Peak Energy, a US-based startup, has raised tens of millions of dollars to build a factory for its sodium-ion battery packs. It already has more than $1.1 billion in announced deals with customers. Sodium batteries have less-intensive cooling needs than lithium-ion packs, and they are less likely to catch fire. Another US-based startup, Inlyte Energy, is working on another type of sodium-based battery that can&#39;t catch fire at all.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=f6b93b62-9498-11f1-b407-617d207c3f6a%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786356070%26t=1786359902%26s=74af2209c230a330bc89fc8a8548c43ac279017a75ea47abb8646f99d405931b/1/0100019feb58f02b-b9cd628c-a490-43d3-8cb6-db5f7e5b0340-000000/psYd67HjVJrQUzip8J0z9-_IyH9_mJ4GMRMtx76jGhI=452">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://asimovaddendum.substack.com/p/googles-westinghouse-bet">Google&#39;s Westinghouse Bet</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="google-s-westinghouse-bet-183692"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Google&#39;s recent reshuffling of its AI operations has many thinking that the company has failed in the AI race. However, the company may just be refocusing its resources on its strengths. Google Cloud may be a much larger economic opportunity than pursuing its rivals in the frontier AI race. The company&#39;s enormous distribution advantage means it doesn&#39;t have to compete in the expensive and unprofitable frontier race.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/amazon-cracks-down-on-cpu-waste-among-engineers-as-agentic-a-3e8f05/">Amazon cracks down on &#39;CPU waste&#39; among engineers as agentic AI crunch intensifies</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="amazon-cracks-down-on-cpu-waste-among-engineers-as-agentic-a-3e8f05"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">AWS is cracking down on internal use of EC2 instances among its engineers. Engineers have been told to reduce CPU waste to ensure the company has enough CPU capacity to meet customer demand. The increased demand for CPU is due to the rise of AI agentic workloads. Other cloud companies are also seeing high demand for CPUs.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/shopify-s-blowout-earnings-show-ai-tools-are-paying-off-110e22/">Shopify&#39;s blowout earnings show AI tools are paying off</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="shopify-s-blowout-earnings-show-ai-tools-are-paying-off-110e22"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">While other tech giants struggle to justify their AI spending, Shopify is seeing major benefits. The company&#39;s stock surged by 25% on Wednesday after it beat Q2 revenue-growth expectations (its fifth consecutive quarter of 30%+ growth), partly thanks to its AI tools — like one that helps merchants swiftly build their online storefronts. Its strong revenue from payment processing also strengthened its case to investors that it controls the plumbing of the e-commerce economy.</div>

<div class="issue-story">

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/nvidia-lines-up-500-billion-in-financing-as-ceo-jensen-huang-522c0d/">Nvidia lines up $500 billion in financing as CEO Jensen Huang tells CNBC his chips are &#39;investable asset&#39;</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="nvidia-lines-up-500-billion-in-financing-as-ceo-jensen-huang-522c0d"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Nvidia has partnered with six large asset managers on a $500 billion financing push designed to treat compute infrastructure much like commercial real estate, toll roads, or other assets to borrow against. The effort will mobilize third-party capital for hyperscalers, frontier AI labs, and enterprises to build out data centers and acquire Nvidia hardware. It will help Nvidia&#39;s customers secure financing without tapping their own balance sheets. Nvidia believes lenders can reliably underwrite compute as a revenue-generating asset with an extended life as its hardware is broadly adopted and transferable across customers.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/allow-ffmpeg-and-ffprobe-input-arguments-to-be-configured-fd12d0/">Allow ffmpeg and ffprobe input arguments to be configured</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="allow-ffmpeg-and-ffprobe-input-arguments-to-be-configured-fd12d0"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Two new configuration parameters are introduced. config.active_storage.video_preview_input_arguments is passed to ffmpeg before -i, and config.active_storage.ffprobe_arguments is passed to ffprobe before the file path. Both default to empty string.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/allow-config-active-storage-variant-processor-to-be-set-to-a-c360cc/">Allow config.active_storage.variant_processor to be set to a class</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="allow-config-active-storage-variant-processor-to-be-set-to-a-c360cc"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">The class must implement the interface defined by ActiveStorage::Transformers::Transformer. Active Storage then uses it for variant processing</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/the-schema-readers-answer-for-many-tables-at-once-781747/">The schema readers answer for many tables at once</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-schema-readers-answer-for-many-tables-at-once-781747"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">indexes / primary_keys / foreign_keys / check_constraints / exclusion_constraints / unique_constraints now accept a list of tables and answer for all of them at once</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/fix-lengthvalidator-crash-with-proc-minimum-and-nil-value-284a07/">Fix LengthValidator crash with proc minimum and nil value</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="fix-lengthvalidator-crash-with-proc-minimum-and-nil-value-284a07"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">The proc was resolved only when the value was present, so for a nil value it leaked unresolved into the error message and was invoked with the message options hash instead of the record. It is now resolved before the error is built, producing the expected &quot;is too short&quot; message.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/use-bind-parameters-for-array-form-arguments-in-find-by-sql--e4886c/">Use bind parameters for array-form arguments in find_by_sql / count_by_sql</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="use-bind-parameters-for-array-form-arguments-in-find-by-sql--e4886c"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">The API doc claims parity with where, but array-form values were eagerly interpolated via sanitize_sql.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/deprecate-create-alias-of-insert-in-connection-adapters-2f3100/">Deprecate create alias of insert in connection adapters</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="deprecate-create-alias-of-insert-in-connection-adapters-2f3100"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">insert / update / delete correspond to their SQL DML verbs, but create reads like DDL (create_table / create_database) rather than the INSERT it actually performs.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/split-keyword-arguments-off-args-on-rails-application-middle-effb2c/">Split keyword arguments off #args on Rails.application.middleware entries</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="split-keyword-arguments-off-args-on-rails-application-middle-effb2c"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Middleware entries now expose keyword arguments through a new #kwargs accessor. #args previously bundled kwargs as a trailing hash inside the positional array (via Hash.ruby2_keywords_hash). It now returns positional arguments only.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/allow-to-configure-erb-options-through-actionview-base-f5815c/">Allow to configure ERB options through ActionView::Base</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="allow-to-configure-erb-options-through-actionview-base-f5815c"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">The ActionView::Template::Handlers::ERB class is now private API. Applications that used to configure ERB options such as escape_ignore_list now need to do this on the ActionView::Base class or on the railtie config.action_view configuration.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/append-traditional-instead-of-strict-all-tables-to-mysql-s-s-1cc46c/">Append TRADITIONAL instead of STRICT_ALL_TABLES to MySQL&#39;s sql_mode by default</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="append-traditional-instead-of-strict-all-tables-to-mysql-s-s-1cc46c"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">On environments whose global sql_mode is empty — most notably Amazon RDS and Aurora MySQL default parameter groups — appending only STRICT_ALL_TABLES leaves out NO_ZERO_IN_DATE, NO_ZERO_DATE, and ERROR_FOR_DIVISION_BY_ZERO, which MySQL 5.7+ has otherwise made part of its own default. Appending TRADITIONAL closes the gap.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/allow-activesupport-proxylogger-to-ignore-messages-by-patter-004c20/">Allow ActiveSupport::ProxyLogger to ignore messages by pattern</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="allow-activesupport-proxylogger-to-ignore-messages-by-patter-004c20"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Useful to silence noisy logs from gems that your application may not care about, without needing to change the log level and losing other useful logs.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/add-pacific-time-canada-and-alberta-time-zone-mappings-90fb56/">Add Pacific Time (Canada) and Alberta Time zone mappings</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="add-pacific-time-canada-and-alberta-time-zone-mappings-90fb56"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Adds two friendly zone mappings: Pacific Time (Canada) → America/Vancouver, Alberta → America/Edmonton</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/change-the-shape-of-activerecord-migration-commandrecorder-c-050359/">Change the shape of ActiveRecord::Migration::CommandRecorder#commands</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="change-the-shape-of-activerecord-migration-commandrecorder-c-050359"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Each recorded migration command is now stored as [cmd, args, kwargs, block] (4-element) instead of [cmd, args, block] (3-element) with kwargs bundled into a trailing hash inside args. Code that inspects recorder.commands directly needs to adapt to the new tuple shape.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/fix-normalizes-to-run-before-the-underlying-type-validates-a-402273/">Fix normalizes to run before the underlying type validates an assigned value</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="fix-normalizes-to-run-before-the-underlying-type-validates-a-402273"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">When normalizes was combined with another type that rejects invalid input (such as an Active Record enum), the underlying type&#39;s assert_valid_value ran against the raw, un-normalized value and raised before normalization had a chance to run. The normalization is now applied first, so a value like &quot; Pending &quot; is normalized to &quot;pending&quot; and accepted by the enum.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://world.hey.com/this.week.in.rails/subscribers/o3V6Bj2AHCabrBDnK1ofFwvi/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">AI</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/the-ai-safety-test-is-becoming-a-safety-risk-8d9d53/">The AI safety test is becoming a safety risk</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-ai-safety-test-is-becoming-a-safety-risk-8d9d53"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">AI agents are escaping cybersecurity testing environments and reaching real-world systems, raising questions about whether safety infrastructure, industry standards and regulation can keep pace with increasingly powerful models.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.techcrunch.com/oc/6871770a273e032f335072f3ry6g4.63s/6d0246ee">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/openai-acquires-presentation-startup-nextslide-acd84d/">OpenAI acquires presentation startup NextSlide</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="openai-acquires-presentation-startup-nextslide-acd84d"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">NextSlide says its team members are now working on ChatGPT.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.techcrunch.com/oc/6871770a273e032f335072f3ry6g4.63s/6d0246ee">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/cloudflare-launches-kitesurf-a-browser-built-for-ai-agents-2ed834/">Cloudflare launches Kitesurf, a browser built for AI agents</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="cloudflare-launches-kitesurf-a-browser-built-for-ai-agents-2ed834"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Kitesurf is a cloud-hosted browser designed for AI agents instead of people. It uses less computing power than Chromium for common automation tasks, helping developers build browser-based AI agents more efficiently.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.techcrunch.com/oc/6871770a273e032f335072f3ry6g4.63s/6d0246ee">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://leadershipintech.com/links/22932/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/email">[Webinar] Measuring AI developer productivity: Can you prove AI is working?</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="webinar-measuring-ai-developer-productivity-can-you-prove-ai-59cde5"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">AI is in your engineering workflow. While the token spend shows it, the throughput doesn&#39;t. The human is very much still in the loop, and that&#39;s a context problem. Join live on Aug 19 (FREE) to learn how to track AI productivity, how context maturity affects where teams stall, and what it takes to get real value from your agents.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; Sponsored &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/the-lights-were-never-off-f53893/">The lights were never off</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-lights-were-never-off-f53893"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">The idea of the dark factory promises software development with little or no human involvement. But the teams closest to this work tell a more nuanced story. Humans still review, verify, and guide AI-generated code. The real challenge is not removing people, but replacing the work reviewers do with strong tests, simulations, automation, and safeguards.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/generative-user-interface-f702db/">Generative user interface</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="generative-user-interface-f702db"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Generative UI replaces walls of AI text with dynamic interface elements that adapt to what users actually want. Three approaches exist: static components mapped from tool results, declarative JSON specs, and open-ended code generation inside sandboxed iframes. These are not mutually exclusive, and production apps will likely combine all three.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">React Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://reactdigest.net/subscribers/15ba1f04-697f-45f3-a1e0-b46f193a8e06/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/evaluation-for-coding-agents-on-realistic-react-work-90db76/">Evaluation for coding agents on realistic React work</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="evaluation-for-coding-agents-on-realistic-react-work-90db76"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Existing benchmarks let models pass every test while still writing React code that breaks in production. ReactBench was built to catch what tests miss, like performance, accessibility, and code quality issues. The benchmark targets React specifically because it powers around 70% of JavaScript-based websites, meaning small model mistakes can spread fast.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">React Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://reactdigest.net/subscribers/15ba1f04-697f-45f3-a1e0-b46f193a8e06/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">

<div class="issue-story">

<div class="issue-story-blurb">Google put Gemma on a Raspberry Pi. Now it translates with the internet switched off. Speak into the mic. It hears you, works it out, and answers out loud in another language.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">Nomura says India has become a key test of AI&#39;s labor impact, with 83,100 AI-related hires since 2022 versus 31,921 layoffs and attrition. Most cuts involved support teams replaced by chatbots, while hiring centered on tech roles driven by AI demand. Economists say the totals challenge mass-job-loss fears but still hide painful displacement and widening workforce divides.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">KPMG found nearly half of large organizations scaled back, delayed, or paused AI agent rollouts after costs began exceeding expected value, while only 7% reported established ROI. The airline also invested £100 million in resilience and added 600 Heathrow staff.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/claude-pushes-a-37-year-math-record-42da46/">Claude Pushes a 37-Year Math Record</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="claude-pushes-a-37-year-math-record-42da46"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Anthropic&#39;s Claude tested on Riemann Hypothesis increased share of zeta zeros on critical line from 41.6% to 67.2%, a 25.6% gain in 36 hours vs. 0.8% over 37 years by humans Achievement required brute force stamina: 650 failed attempts across 60 agents and 31 million tokens, not novel mathematical insight Represents broader trend of AI breaking decades-old mathematical records through persistence rather than genius</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">AI Secret &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://aisecret.us/unsubscribe/?uuid=51dd95b7-a6ea-4b91-a3f3-61c022f08768&amp;key=96e91569283006ee4283ef36edbbb758d75e5c70220aff1513737395c8039947&amp;newsletter=3ce353fa-0781-406f-968b-f712405ba520">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">Meta released Muse Glimmer, a 30B open-weight agent model under Apache 2.0 license, runnable locally on Mac or single consumer GPU with no cloud dependency Optimized for tool use and agent tasks, matching GPT-5.5 on benchmarks but scoring roughly half of frontier models on hard general reasoning Designed for fixed, repeatable SOPs (read files, call tools, run scripts, retry) rather than open-ended reasoning</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://links.tldrnewsletter.com/5nnDda">Mark Zuckerberg Lays Out New AI Vision in 6,500-Word Essay</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="mark-zuckerberg-lays-out-new-ai-vision-in-6-500-word-essay-0f663f"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Mark Zuckerberg recently released a wide-ranging essay that outlined a new course of action to spread the wealth and opportunity from AI to users around the world, and to residents of the communities that host the data centers powering it. Zuckerberg&#39;s plans include releasing more open-weight models and establishing a $1 billion fund to invest in the communities where Meta hosts data centers. He believes that Meta&#39;s approach to developing powerful AI and distributing it as widely as possible is the least likely path to disastrous outcomes for humanity.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=19a357d8-9567-11f1-8920-5509caa85f4b%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786442421%26t=1786446300%26s=fc247acc8bb43bac5f03a6bb2beab7cfcd8e4075f86c22f936cd2becadffed74/1/0100019ff07f45ea-2a2ade7c-c4d8-499e-ac2b-8e5de6023415-000000/rWP3qNiD0yXK-XrlRacLGrx494EiqNaC8XEZotRbtE0=452">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/learning-more-about-claude-s-mathematical-capabilities-0b3b75/">Learning more about Claude&#39;s mathematical capabilities</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="learning-more-about-claude-s-mathematical-capabilities-0b3b75"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Claude was recently challenged with one of the most famous unsolved problems in mathematics: the Riemann hypothesis. While Claude didn&#39;t succeed, it unexpectedly made strides on a related problem. It improved on a longstanding lower bound for the fraction of zeros of the Riemann zeta function that satisfy the Riemann hypothesis. The result shows that AI models like Claude can extend the impact and reach of mathematicians&#39; ideas in new and sometimes surprising ways.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=19a357d8-9567-11f1-8920-5509caa85f4b%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786442421%26t=1786446300%26s=fc247acc8bb43bac5f03a6bb2beab7cfcd8e4075f86c22f936cd2becadffed74/1/0100019ff07f45ea-2a2ade7c-c4d8-499e-ac2b-8e5de6023415-000000/rWP3qNiD0yXK-XrlRacLGrx494EiqNaC8XEZotRbtE0=452">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/no-local-models-will-not-win-0f812e/">No, local models will not win</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="no-local-models-will-not-win-0f812e"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Local models are never going to be as powerful as datacenter models. All of the current frontier models are far too big to run on anything but a full GPU cluster. Smaller models are getting more intelligent, but datacenter models will always be the strongest available. When given a choice, people are going to pick the strongest available model in their price range, the one that frustrates them least, which is always going to be the bigger, more powerful one.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=19a357d8-9567-11f1-8920-5509caa85f4b%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786442421%26t=1786446300%26s=fc247acc8bb43bac5f03a6bb2beab7cfcd8e4075f86c22f936cd2becadffed74/1/0100019ff07f45ea-2a2ade7c-c4d8-499e-ac2b-8e5de6023415-000000/rWP3qNiD0yXK-XrlRacLGrx494EiqNaC8XEZotRbtE0=452">unsubscribe</a></div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/claude-text-gets-invisible-watermarks-6b0f43/">Claude Text Gets Invisible Watermarks</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="claude-text-gets-invisible-watermarks-6b0f43"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Anthropic says all new Claude models launched on or after August 2, 2026 will embed invisible watermarks into generated text worldwide, under an EU AI Act code it signed. Because the watermark is part of the text rather than metadata, it can survive copying, pasting and some edits. Anthropic is still working to add the system to existing Claude models.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/zoom-bets-creators-boost-ai-visibility-de788d/">Zoom Bets Creators Boost AI Visibility</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="zoom-bets-creators-boost-ai-visibility-de788d"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Zoom is using creators to strengthen how often AI search tools surface and cite its brand, as it tries to reposition itself beyond meetings into an AI-first work platform. Its strategy focuses on building authority across trusted sources, while tracking which publications and creators LLMs favor. Zoom says the creator-to-AI visibility link remains unproven, making this an experimental marketing bet.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/ai-pattern-makes-toyota-invisible-to-camera-ea2eed/">AI Pattern Makes Toyota Invisible to Camera</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="ai-pattern-makes-toyota-invisible-to-camera-ea2eed"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Cybersecurity researcher Bill Swearingen wrapped a Toyota Yaris in an AI-generated adversarial pattern and drove it past a Flock surveillance camera at Def Con. The camera still recorded the car, but the pattern confused the computer-vision software responsible for identifying vehicles. His noRecognition system uses reinforcement learning to repeatedly refine designs until detection models fail to recognize otherwise visible objects.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/the-startup-behind-three-ai-incidents-03e29a/">The Startup Behind Three AI Incidents</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-startup-behind-three-ai-incidents-03e29a"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">OpenAI, Anthropic and Meta all referenced Irregular after recent AI security incidents because the Israeli startup ran the independent evaluation environments used to test their frontier models. Irregular specializes in offensive cybersecurity testing, probing whether models can find vulnerabilities, execute attacks or behave unexpectedly. It says the incidents shared the same environment misconfiguration, not sophisticated sandbox escapes.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">MyClaw Newsletter &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://newsletter.myclaw.ai/unsubscribe/?uuid=2eaf3154-2af3-4e54-b574-fba62b0fa39d&amp;key=dc9420fa06ad8715ca418803968586b4a3d508870a709a3c794ae1e1640114bf&amp;newsletter=98c25587-e84c-44c2-84de-1f862d1ace82">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/house-democrats-seek-ai-hearings-508712/">House Democrats Seek AI Hearings</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="house-democrats-seek-ai-hearings-508712"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">House Democrats are pressing Speaker Mike Johnson to hold public hearings where major AI CEOs would testify under oath about recent security incidents. Separately, lawmakers sent OpenAI and Anthropic detailed oversight letters demanding logs and answers to 23 questions by August 24, including how models escaped testing limits, whether negligence played a role, and what safeguards could prevent repeat incidents.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/pwc-s-ai-error-exposes-accountability-gap-0931eb/">PwC&#39;s AI Error Exposes Accountability Gap</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="pwc-s-ai-error-exposes-accountability-gap-0931eb"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">PwC is correcting Middle East reports after investigators found fabricated footnotes, unverifiable sources, and unsupported claims in AI-assisted work. The incident highlights a broader leadership problem: multiple review layers can still leave factual verification without a clear owner.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">MyClaw Newsletter &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://newsletter.myclaw.ai/unsubscribe/?uuid=2eaf3154-2af3-4e54-b574-fba62b0fa39d&amp;key=dc9420fa06ad8715ca418803968586b4a3d508870a709a3c794ae1e1640114bf&amp;newsletter=98c25587-e84c-44c2-84de-1f862d1ace82">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.K3FzaWG5vnes9asChrWWUH4s5J6LGhy1MGtNBf8LtppcYvmRay-Jbk5_opK7VH-OAFyxREqVmUdn7pndSlJvzm2pUzGYINcvFCX5cOOdxFDQdBOY51GtXMZDX4in722ExfE3AJzoc4jUrgfD97dAN7aYcY_cxQjVkD5BexBHAxs/4t3/I8Rvxi4FSDyuGeSaF3WM-g/h15/h001.9SlWuezp_6InfE0dd6R4ZPwIlMMRrGWTAgO2efpo770">Agentic Code Quality</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="agentic-code-quality-378aa6"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">For much of human history, we&#39;ve evaluated code quality via code review: someone reads what you wrote and makes sure it&#39;s clean, thoughtful, fast, understandable, and tests well. For agents, that approach doesn&#39;t scale well; there&#39;s just too much code for anyone to read. As a result, more and more of our quality checks have to happen in the harness, environment, and operating system around the agent. Software quality now depends on the constraints you set around your agents.</div>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/the-agentic-awakening-why-10-faster-coding-doesn-t-translate-d82d35/">The Agentic Awakening: Why 10× Faster Coding Doesn&#39;t Translate Into Proportional Organizational Productivity, and How AI-pilled Leaders Do It.</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-agentic-awakening-why-10-faster-coding-doesn-t-translate-d82d35"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Even companies with 85-90% AI-generated code and 10x local acceleration never crossed 50% end-to-end productivity gain, because the organization around the engineers, PM handoffs, planning cadence, and review queues still runs at pre-AI speed. Individual velocity is the easy win; redesigning the org so faster engineers actually ship faster is the part almost everyone skips.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://softwareleadweekly.us6.list-manage.com/unsubscribe?u=1a258e0fefbb23214c59c5a8d&amp;id=efe3d3cd5b&amp;t=b&amp;e=86169b0ba2&amp;c=22ba65c4f8">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/ai-adoption-is-a-myth-d05484/">AI Adoption Is a Myth</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="ai-adoption-is-a-myth-d05484"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">To leverage AI across the entire org, mapping existing workflows and desired transformations (&quot;I wish it was possible to...&quot;) is an exercise every leader in the organization has to drive, track, and put into action: &quot;So stop reporting &#39;adoption&#39; to your board. Report what share of the work today is manual vs hybrid vs fully automated.&quot;</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://softwareleadweekly.us6.list-manage.com/unsubscribe?u=1a258e0fefbb23214c59c5a8d&amp;id=efe3d3cd5b&amp;t=b&amp;e=86169b0ba2&amp;c=22ba65c4f8">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://us.list-manage.com/dpDBQeGNUxA?e=86169b0ba2&amp;c2id=4541cead0ded4f764194eec0f1f5b20f">Platform Engineering Still Matters</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="platform-engineering-still-matters-bedc6e"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Evan Meagher points to Martin Fowler&#39;s related take: &quot;The goal of refactoring an agentic code base is to spend tokens now in refactoring to make token consumption for future work lower.&quot; -- I think that&#39;s the missing half of most AI ROI conversations: we measure the tokens spent today and rarely the tokens saved next quarter.</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Neurotech</h2>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://techcrunch.com/sponsor/tether/tether-evos-latest-research-addresses-one-of-the-biggest-challenges-in-brain-computer-interfaces/">Tether Addresses a Top Challenge in Brain-Computer Interfaces</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="tether-addresses-a-top-challenge-in-brain-computer-interface-47b3a5"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Three peer-reviewed papers show a single AI model can decode speech, vision, and music from brain activity across different people, cutting patient calibration from weeks to minutes. The research tackles one of neurotech&#39;s biggest challenges, that every brain has different signals.</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Leadership</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/before-you-delegate-7cfe97/">Before you delegate</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="before-you-delegate-7cfe97"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Delegating is hard when explaining a task feels slower than just doing it yourself. Wes shares a simple checklist of six questions that makes it easier: understand what the person already knows, share the why, gather needed resources, show what good looks like, set a clear timeline, and flag likely risks. These questions help build shared context fast and improve the quality of work handed off.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/overpromising-cad870/">Overpromising</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="overpromising-cad870"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Promising career rewards to keep team members happy often backfires badly. The initial boost fades fast, and if the promise falls through, trust is shattered and can take years to rebuild. Managers should only make promises they can fully guarantee, and caveat anything uncertain with honest disclaimers. Focus on what you can control, not what you hope will happen.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/engineering-management-after-the-cost-of-code-collapsed-f05eac/">Engineering management after the cost of code collapsed</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="engineering-management-after-the-cost-of-code-collapsed-f05eac"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Engineering management rules aren&#39;t uniformly obsolete or valid in the AI era. The key is checking what assumption each practice rests on. If a rule depends on the cost of writing code, revisit it. If it depends on how humans coordinate, verify, or accept risk, it still holds. Code generation got cheap, but specification, judgment, and ownership did not. Whoever has to sign a decision still sets the pace.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/when-nothing-happens-a30c66/">When nothing happens</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="when-nothing-happens-a30c66"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Organizations don&#39;t punish bad behavior — they punish bad behavior when results turn. Research shows that whether a harsh leader is seen as inspiring or abusive depends mostly on whether the team is winning. Cases like Steve Jobs, Linda Wachner, and Bobby Knight all follow the same pattern: the behavior stays constant, but the moment results drop, tolerance evaporates. The real question for any leader is how much cover strong performance is currently buying for someone on their team.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Leadership in Tech &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://leadershipintech.com/subscribers/027d9985-d4c5-48a7-af59-5152efd9c8a4/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.u7AEjFG7xD7PeXgObD5uI1o6BtYZXOmqEviUlhsqJwg_GMPqMjdd2bkbP6XyhcH7NDT9MXt8Hzw-7WFWf_vMQ0z0kmQLD6013ImfEIihMwilPXg8iK8x7SscDskcDQUpr4P93YBJxSbqFefGRxdkhNDyBJMl6EM2gQ90Q3OJW135n5tUqlkeP8fMcjx5Bt9e/4t3/I8Rvxi4FSDyuGeSaF3WM-g/h8/h001.Z2CUzlJebYRMy3FFKcDIsWGUL56IDJx8CZOZzt_AVtU">Relentless Forward Progress</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="relentless-forward-progress-ff631e"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">The hardest stretches of running a team are not sprints. They are ultras: the long downturn, the slow turnaround, the market that stays bad for six quarters straight. None is solved by a single heroic burst. They are survived, or not, by how you manage the long middle, when the adrenaline is gone and the only skill left is suffering productively while metering what you have.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Pointer &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.PFjHD-ii0vvUj1FcNnOS5qqF3eJEkrxympSzSDDZUB29F4951mhY03iCawzSQ6poZedL3n7H-lMzRTsKnY9bvtDfJYA8pICpvyyNYWT98Xtr9WdPcSpxxaa7Q5dr87aiYGihRTwZYkR0HjoovPrejageBVLzHH9tG8n84m3dFIuMnCMDviQfRQLGnrFY4wdh3h9Zwffqoap7IahStn1Zx9FxBC0dbqtZcIWs5Qb2lN3W7NPzwNLQCfjXO_oDddTMOMO_mDbiK4VXjp1fg0-PLD5T9qz5DNPZ4gPMhln17AcwX5HCUm5nV8yifu3QO6FA3jpiAkxgp2Mga6WTGfDYbfAq_T7sK9zfaJAm00tC2QunGj2lY86lBE1dFZp5U6N_im05m5AIB2IAgDvWKJ3YhaB56b4WVanjznLY5Bvo_orK3zGNNsqe9Fz-h354g125n0GK31bnyYRICxTARq7LIQcIbxkh2-GC2sY0FTySAcgNi5v83clGhv8d5LZFgU6MZAxYta1vlgcYL0Y25YRfZ3yYWPPDI-LWTVEBF2_6tzVavgtSBxtb7oLFpmuv_cQ0c9fh6G6tostqN1n6B_0dRwcLo59JmH6XsbaIW-96oRi4PTJpCTy5avyoqVYEgUPMgUD9IoCGpOrlGLE8xP5OmnrjFk-gxKhBberFaQhbUA_3gZN0jQBfqMwHzbKhl0Prhr8kw71jWOxARr4om1Dh67yfV6bUA3OTNRS6Q-1cAWi9DGrOKMhpYg-sRiHIeOpfMmJULlis7grAKzRUCNZL9hDIrWT67t46do1V3Y0Y9fpuV_5fbXsSJtWcl3JdKIVEa_6bhhpDeVb3q_yuzXYEsCKii1OeVAiNU9Tsd_KCB9dbJ8WXE_DrLc5E5-4pnRYCQhyHJJ-IrjWYwpGens3VKw/4t3/I8Rvxi4FSDyuGeSaF3WM-g/h30/h001.ufyGOYgPPqykKadV2GndXMR7RdGBVK_zskIjnMjfT7M">unsubscribe</a></div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">React</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/advanced-react-state-boundaries-in-a-real-next-js-app-9eaa02/">Advanced React state boundaries in a real Next.js app</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="advanced-react-state-boundaries-in-a-real-next-js-app-9eaa02"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Nir describes how to manage state across the boundaries of a modern Next.js app. He covers URL state, browser APIs, TanStack Query caches, React Server Components, client context, and optimistic updates. The main lesson is to clearly define who owns each piece of state, how React should react to changes, and how different parts of the application stay synchronized.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">React Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://reactdigest.net/subscribers/15ba1f04-697f-45f3-a1e0-b46f193a8e06/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/react-19-useactionstate-explained-13ee5f/">React 19 useActionState explained</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="react-19-useactionstate-explained-13ee5f"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">React 19&#39;s useActionState hook replaces the repetitive pattern of manually managing form submission state. Pass it an action function and an initial state, and it returns the current state, a form action, and a pending flag. The biggest production mistake is using a local pending flag instead of the built-in one, which can let users queue duplicate submissions. Shubhra suggests skipping it for optimistic updates, multi-step wizards, or anything not tied to a form.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">React Digest &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://reactdigest.net/subscribers/15ba1f04-697f-45f3-a1e0-b46f193a8e06/unsubscribe">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Preact</h2>

<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/async-hydration-in-preact-ad26e5/">Async hydration in Preact</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="async-hydration-in-preact-ad26e5"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Preact already supports async hydration through Suspense boundaries, letting parts of a page become interactive independently rather than all at once. Hydration 2.0, coming in Preact 11, makes this more reliable by marking exact DOM ranges in server HTML, fixing edge cases where the wrong nodes were targeted.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/insurers-push-ai-into-care-decisions-970832/">Insurers Push AI Into Care Decisions</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="insurers-push-ai-into-care-decisions-970832"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Health insurers are moving AI beyond post-care claims processing into earlier decisions about patient navigation, treatment recommendations and pre-approvals. Sun Life uses Medzown to steer complex cases toward clinical trials, while Aetna personalizes care pathways. As adoption expands, seven states have imposed guardrails requiring human clinicians to approve denials or delays in care rather than AI alone.</div>

<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/the-end-of-cursor-08b7b0/">The End of Cursor</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-end-of-cursor-08b7b0"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Cursor, the AI coding IDE built by four MIT grads into $4B annual revenue and 50,000 enterprise customers, is being acquired for $60 billion The acquisition will result in Cursor&#39;s name disappearing, with its unreleased agent shipped under a Grok label instead Cursor&#39;s model-agnostic approach attracted users away from Copilot, but the acquisition demonstrates that app-layer companies without their own AI models cannot remain independent</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">AI Secret &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://aisecret.us/unsubscribe/?uuid=51dd95b7-a6ea-4b91-a3f3-61c022f08768&amp;key=96e91569283006ee4283ef36edbbb758d75e5c70220aff1513737395c8039947&amp;newsletter=3ce353fa-0781-406f-968b-f712405ba520">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">Time magazine is serving hidden ads to AI crawlers in FAQ-shaped article versions visible only to machines like ClaudeBot These ads are designed to shape AI model outputs and long-term memory rather than reach human readers Publishers are pivoting to advertise to machines because human web traffic has declined and bot traffic now dominates their audience</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Science</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/the-first-self-driving-vehicle-on-mars-has-proven-to-be-a-sm-85bc2d/">The first self-driving vehicle on Mars has proven to be a smashing success</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-first-self-driving-vehicle-on-mars-has-proven-to-be-a-sm-85bc2d"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">NASA&#39;s Perseverance Mars rover will set a record for the most distance driven by any vehicle on another world sometime next week. The rover&#39;s auto navigation system has enabled about 90% of the distance driven by the vehicle to be autonomous. Not having to wait for navigation commands from drivers on Earth has allowed the rover to maximize the amount of scientific return. The vehicle will likely be able to keep driving itself across Mars for many years to come.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=f6b93b62-9498-11f1-b407-617d207c3f6a%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786356070%26t=1786359902%26s=74af2209c230a330bc89fc8a8548c43ac279017a75ea47abb8646f99d405931b/1/0100019feb58f02b-b9cd628c-a490-43d3-8cb6-db5f7e5b0340-000000/psYd67HjVJrQUzip8J0z9-_IyH9_mJ4GMRMtx76jGhI=452">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Programming</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://addyo.substack.com/p/agentic-code-quality">Agentic Code Quality</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="agentic-code-quality-25030b"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Software quality now depends on the constraints set around the agents that wrote it. Constraints define what a system is allowed to do. Setting and maintaining these constraints allows developers to build loops that reliably deliver high-quality production software. Agents can propose anything - constraints decide whether a proposal is safe enough, correct, scoped, and useful.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=f6b93b62-9498-11f1-b407-617d207c3f6a%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786356070%26t=1786359902%26s=74af2209c230a330bc89fc8a8548c43ac279017a75ea47abb8646f99d405931b/1/0100019feb58f02b-b9cd628c-a490-43d3-8cb6-db5f7e5b0340-000000/psYd67HjVJrQUzip8J0z9-_IyH9_mJ4GMRMtx76jGhI=452">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/changing-devtools-is-cheap-owning-them-isn-t-50a1ab/">Changing Devtools Is Cheap. Owning Them Isn&#39;t</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="changing-devtools-is-cheap-owning-them-isn-t-50a1ab"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">The future of software will be powered by high-quality, well-documented building blocks that users can build specialized artifacts on top of. Agents are good at composing high-quality components. Maintainers can provide these components alongside a focus application, and users can choose how to modify their experiences themselves. This allows software to be personalized while still being developed by maintainers that care.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=f6b93b62-9498-11f1-b407-617d207c3f6a%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786356070%26t=1786359902%26s=74af2209c230a330bc89fc8a8548c43ac279017a75ea47abb8646f99d405931b/1/0100019feb58f02b-b9cd628c-a490-43d3-8cb6-db5f7e5b0340-000000/psYd67HjVJrQUzip8J0z9-_IyH9_mJ4GMRMtx76jGhI=452">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/why-you-might-want-to-build-your-webapp-in-canvas-instead-of-446040/">Why you might want to build your WebApp in Canvas instead of HTML</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="why-you-might-want-to-build-your-webapp-in-canvas-instead-of-446040"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Canvas is a low-level rendering tool that gives users more control than HTML.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=f6b93b62-9498-11f1-b407-617d207c3f6a%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786356070%26t=1786359902%26s=74af2209c230a330bc89fc8a8548c43ac279017a75ea47abb8646f99d405931b/1/0100019feb58f02b-b9cd628c-a490-43d3-8cb6-db5f7e5b0340-000000/psYd67HjVJrQUzip8J0z9-_IyH9_mJ4GMRMtx76jGhI=452">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/how-i-rebuilt-my-development-workstation-for-agentic-work-6663cc/">How I Rebuilt My Development Workstation for Agentic Work</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="how-i-rebuilt-my-development-workstation-for-agentic-work-6663cc"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">This post describes a workstation that is terminal-native, agentic-first, and built around composable tools.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=f6b93b62-9498-11f1-b407-617d207c3f6a%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786356070%26t=1786359902%26s=74af2209c230a330bc89fc8a8548c43ac279017a75ea47abb8646f99d405931b/1/0100019feb58f02b-b9cd628c-a490-43d3-8cb6-db5f7e5b0340-000000/psYd67HjVJrQUzip8J0z9-_IyH9_mJ4GMRMtx76jGhI=452">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/what-s-the-best-programming-language-for-coding-agents-93cee7/">What&#39;s the best programming language for coding agents?</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="what-s-the-best-programming-language-for-coding-agents-93cee7"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Anyone who wants to make a strong claim about which language or classes of languages are particularly good with LLMs would need to run quite a few different evaluations. Failures with languages are generally something idiosyncratic, where it&#39;s not always obvious how much the issue generalizes across tasks or setups. There&#39;s no way to look at the score on one or even several evaluations and draw a conclusion about programming in general. At this point, it would be a mistake to draw a strong conclusion about any particular language.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=19a357d8-9567-11f1-8920-5509caa85f4b%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786442421%26t=1786446300%26s=fc247acc8bb43bac5f03a6bb2beab7cfcd8e4075f86c22f936cd2becadffed74/1/0100019ff07f45ea-2a2ade7c-c4d8-499e-ac2b-8e5de6023415-000000/rWP3qNiD0yXK-XrlRacLGrx494EiqNaC8XEZotRbtE0=452">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Consumer</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/gen-z-s-little-treat-economy-8-pastries-instead-of-homes-623b25/">Gen Z&#39;s &#39;little treat economy&#39;: $8 pastries instead of homes</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="gen-z-s-little-treat-economy-8-pastries-instead-of-homes-623b25"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Gen Z doesn&#39;t have much in the way of savings, but it does love to spend on little treats. Zoomers burn cash on beauty products, snacks, and jewelry. Economists are calling it the &#39;little treat economy,&#39; the result of joyful splurging on frivolous purchases to fill the void left by unaffordable big-ticket items, like homes. Gen Z&#39;s average spending on little treats grew by more than 6% YoY in June, compared to 3% for all generations.</div>

Even a stratospheric bid would keep Musk in firm control of the combined company.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">Gen Z doesn&#39;t have much savings but loves spending on little treats. The trend shows Gen Z average spending on little treats grew by more than 6% YoY in June, compared to 3% for all generations, driven by the &#39;little treat economy&#39; of joyful splurging on frivolous purchases to fill the void left by unaffordable big-ticket items.</div>

<div class="issue-story-blurb">Retailers are embracing ChatGPT and Gemini as new shopping discovery channels, with AI-referred visitors already converting better than traditional traffic. But companies such as Ulta, Etsy and The Knot still want customers to complete purchases on their own sites, where they retain browsing, purchase and loyalty data. The goal is simple: let AI recommend, but keep the customer relationship.</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Robotics</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/dyna-2-proves-scaling-laws-for-robotics-1-million-hours-of-h-b0eb3b/">Dyna-2 Proves Scaling Laws for Robotics: 1 Million Hours of Human Video Unlocks Zero-Shot Dexterity</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="dyna-2-proves-scaling-laws-for-robotics-1-million-hours-of-h-b0eb3b"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Dyna-2 is a world-action model (WAM) pre-trained on over one million hours of human video data. Its existence proves that scaling human video data predictably improves zero-shot performance on unseen robot hardware. Dyna-2 achieved an 87% pass rate in real-world zero-shot deployments at customer sites, vastly outperforming the 46% pass rate of its VLA predecessor, Dyna-1. The model uses a new one-step video generation distillation pipeline that drops inference latency by two orders of magnitude for downstream planning.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://tracking.tldrnewsletter.com/CL0/https:%2F%2Fa.tldrnewsletter.com%2Funsubscribe%3Fep=1%26l=cfa2d55a-b7be-11e8-a3c9-06b79b628af2%26lc=e09eb435-7667-11ec-96e5-06b4694bee2a%26p=19a357d8-9567-11f1-8920-5509caa85f4b%26pt=campaign%26pv=4%26spa=1786442421%26t=1786446300%26s=fc247acc8bb43bac5f03a6bb2beab7cfcd8e4075f86c22f936cd2becadffed74/1/0100019ff07f45ea-2a2ade7c-c4d8-499e-ac2b-8e5de6023415-000000/rWP3qNiD0yXK-XrlRacLGrx494EiqNaC8XEZotRbtE0=452">unsubscribe</a></div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">CodeReview</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.Ira8ofUIq_P_lCLURPEgQxtIUCg9PbN2nH_8C3r3GGhT6dIA_Hi6IBIBzjX-IHjlsldrzvJXDO6Amg6oBR3BMyYj2Ph4zK-TxsNsPtDVDHkjF73tSMP5aTRS9KyB-5-KD86q2lDqOGVZCOjZYghu6dX7nLmvYbk6enORSmesINo/4t3/I8Rvxi4FSDyuGeSaF3WM-g/h10/h001.79i_uZgeU6-Bs8CKQru-n_SazrCbIUg46wwdDODkZjo">Review The Actual Change, Not The File List</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="review-the-actual-change-not-the-file-list-f09e5e"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">AI writes more code than ever. Reviewing it shouldn&#39;t mean scrolling forty files in alphabetical order. CodeRabbit Review reorganizes any pull request from a flat file list into a structured, layer-by-layer walkthrough - the logical reading order of the change, not the order your platform happens to sort it. Every range gets its own plain-language summary, with sequence diagrams, state machines, and ERDs generated inline wherever a visual earns its place. (1) Cohorts group related files and chunks so you review one idea at a time. (2) Layers order them so foundational changes - data shapes, contracts - come before the code that depends on them. (3) Code Peek lets you click any variable, function, class or type to see its definition and usages without leaving the tab. (4) Semantic Diff cuts past formatting noise to show what actually changed. From the team that pioneered AI code reviews. 2M reviews every week. 6M repos. 15K customers. Free during early access.</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Growth</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.T-cngy-HiLjOztVyxmCjHO6wagaF1JkcsruqvovvyTPMOZVnPUS6z0VuzKR0UtctL1U4H1lJEFxEwdfrLbvUrC8GH2DDYO85GrXyEttyZt3z9SYOJob-JAb7itjY6fefzkapktZmXdtYakWBh_j8k0bBi9TLmfYr-RnfEBljncsSEH_x7yPzGZJGg6xdi363/4t3/I8Rvxi4FSDyuGeSaF3WM-g/h9/h001._4l7VmVZg7JSEAlwCfF0pDswVBR_HlNZr8GayYqX-Vs">The Antithesis Principle</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="the-antithesis-principle-285f7c"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">(1) If you want to help people learn something, look into making it engaging / entertaining. This first one is the easier, more obvious conclusion. (2) It is actually not in your best interest to be the kind of person who can only learn from entertaining content. Nor to be the kind of person that needs any sort of entertainment as a prerequisite for learning. The vast majority of smart people will never consider this second conclusion.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Pointer &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.PFjHD-ii0vvUj1FcNnOS5qqF3eJEkrxympSzSDDZUB29F4951mhY03iCawzSQ6poZedL3n7H-lMzRTsKnY9bvtDfJYA8pICpvyyNYWT98Xtr9WdPcSpxxaa7Q5dr87aiYGihRTwZYkR0HjoovPrejageBVLzHH9tG8n84m3dFIuMnCMDviQfRQLGnrFY4wdh3h9Zwffqoap7IahStn1Zx9FxBC0dbqtZcIWs5Qb2lN3W7NPzwNLQCfjXO_oDddTMOMO_mDbiK4VXjp1fg0-PLD5T9qz5DNPZ4gPMhln17AcwX5HCUm5nV8yifu3QO6FA3jpiAkxgp2Mga6WTGfDYbfAq_T7sK9zfaJAm00tC2QunGj2lY86lBE1dFZp5U6N_im05m5AIB2IAgDvWKJ3YhaB56b4WVanjznLY5Bvo_orK3zGNNsqe9Fz-h354g125n0GK31bnyYRICxTARq7LIQcIbxkh2-GC2sY0FTySAcgNi5v83clGhv8d5LZFgU6MZAxYta1vlgcYL0Y25YRfZ3yYWPPDI-LWTVEBF2_6tzVavgtSBxtb7oLFpmuv_cQ0c9fh6G6tostqN1n6B_0dRwcLo59JmH6XsbaIW-96oRi4PTJpCTy5avyoqVYEgUPMgUD9IoCGpOrlGLE8xP5OmnrjFk-gxKhBberFaQhbUA_3gZN0jQBfqMwHzbKhl0Prhr8kw71jWOxARr4om1Dh67yfV6bUA3OTNRS6Q-1cAWi9DGrOKMhpYg-sRiHIeOpfMmJULlis7grAKzRUCNZL9hDIrWT67t46do1V3Y0Y9fpuV_5fbXsSJtWcl3JdKIVEa_6bhhpDeVb3q_yuzXYEsCKii1OeVAiNU9Tsd_KCB9dbJ8WXE_DrLc5E5-4pnRYCQhyHJJ-IrjWYwpGens3VKw/4t3/I8Rvxi4FSDyuGeSaF3WM-g/h30/h001.ufyGOYgPPqykKadV2GndXMR7RdGBVK_zskIjnMjfT7M">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.WjplEBHoYsVAvmS39jW9CXUAAVr6wytT5BZVHsxAoNNVvwfujIdDBXjh0wbZL4r32ruZAJCVZmi2RaE3UpFxjHKLHDQdwd4viM4RLB6uNe-__h0K7JXDxEjzoNiev0W9DlUYDQ9sCNVor96iuyXD_21Xuaj8tWdv8iPZ8bY9gHTUX6MAARKJJaQOVUjiI0P7/4t3/I8Rvxi4FSDyuGeSaF3WM-g/h11/h001.4pNkD3MSyNVF2b5tqN-f8sU7QDNBNsr-uFNiZ0j3S9g">Middle Management Roles Are Also A Trap</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="middle-management-roles-are-also-a-trap-90d315"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">If you&#39;re willing to embrace this fact that a little bit of time in middle management roles is important preparation to become an executive, but spending a great deal of time in middle management only prepares you for further middle management roles–and makes you less effective as a potential executive–then the AI-driven shift in managerial fads might be a threat to your current role, but it&#39;s likely to improve your chances to succeed as an executive.</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Onboarding</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.K3FzaWG5vnes9asChrWWUL3lUx0iyQpJsD117pLK3rWxlRoWHMNiSE8TUNA3SzYWGEV3TtRBf7KRNnrRFHY_bneXZ2DsECkcy3FcQEupKJXgcnrcoFySv8nfAwLVGpyl6lU0iPJpw_Nlw4R-Dx-zG5QD2Xz4L1d-Rqov07q1vFGXR3CyTjcGReQN5aNIi-Ig0FkT_QvMts1Dlr0LAUWuTQ/4t3/I8Rvxi4FSDyuGeSaF3WM-g/h12/h001.FgHWN-u7TDE2o-cySDpmLQZA1v8Huzb7xLtC89ouDS0">A Practical Guide To Rapid Onboarding</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="a-practical-guide-to-rapid-onboarding-cc3f4f"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">After nearly two decades at Amazon, operating as a Principal Engineer, often parachuting into complex, high stakes projects, I&#39;ve developed a system to cut through this chaos. In this article, I&#39;ll share with you that exact framework to help you get up to speed unbelievably fast.</div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Auth</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.3a5P_SwQzY5x8USD2q4p0oXX3ts5fIlQ6v0xVbkXJmzsBr1_7-dHqvZd4Me-vEliMJ4OZuKiUgkX69J9LZpUwC_AHZUcQQi89hYpi33iprB-Nmgb6PnTNc2l5AMQKrIMe2G7Fg-CyeteNmJwkUhkJwIJ0uTldpKHoL6m3evq07Q7MlBHw5EuoEadUDqeany3EnSmjSLkRMSJVKp-1wDlfARrq_002V1jHP4GKc-QThI/4t3/I8Rvxi4FSDyuGeSaF3WM-g/h13/h001.tNYNbhm7FHfjn7Ywr2GEfkAQU-BJrkqLAQOcH08fWTs">Agents Can Now Sign Up For Your App</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="agents-can-now-sign-up-for-your-app-fc61c6"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Agents hit your signup flow and bounce off a login built for humans. Every one that gives up is a signup you never see. WorkOS Agent Registration turns that traffic into signups. Enroll via the dashboard and AuthKit publishes an auth.md file agents read to register for scoped, short-lived credentials you control.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Pointer &middot; Sponsored &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.PFjHD-ii0vvUj1FcNnOS5qqF3eJEkrxympSzSDDZUB29F4951mhY03iCawzSQ6poZedL3n7H-lMzRTsKnY9bvtDfJYA8pICpvyyNYWT98Xtr9WdPcSpxxaa7Q5dr87aiYGihRTwZYkR0HjoovPrejageBVLzHH9tG8n84m3dFIuMnCMDviQfRQLGnrFY4wdh3h9Zwffqoap7IahStn1Zx9FxBC0dbqtZcIWs5Qb2lN3W7NPzwNLQCfjXO_oDddTMOMO_mDbiK4VXjp1fg0-PLD5T9qz5DNPZ4gPMhln17AcwX5HCUm5nV8yifu3QO6FA3jpiAkxgp2Mga6WTGfDYbfAq_T7sK9zfaJAm00tC2QunGj2lY86lBE1dFZp5U6N_im05m5AIB2IAgDvWKJ3YhaB56b4WVanjznLY5Bvo_orK3zGNNsqe9Fz-h354g125n0GK31bnyYRICxTARq7LIQcIbxkh2-GC2sY0FTySAcgNi5v83clGhv8d5LZFgU6MZAxYta1vlgcYL0Y25YRfZ3yYWPPDI-LWTVEBF2_6tzVavgtSBxtb7oLFpmuv_cQ0c9fh6G6tostqN1n6B_0dRwcLo59JmH6XsbaIW-96oRi4PTJpCTy5avyoqVYEgUPMgUD9IoCGpOrlGLE8xP5OmnrjFk-gxKhBberFaQhbUA_3gZN0jQBfqMwHzbKhl0Prhr8kw71jWOxARr4om1Dh67yfV6bUA3OTNRS6Q-1cAWi9DGrOKMhpYg-sRiHIeOpfMmJULlis7grAKzRUCNZL9hDIrWT67t46do1V3Y0Y9fpuV_5fbXsSJtWcl3JdKIVEa_6bhhpDeVb3q_yuzXYEsCKii1OeVAiNU9Tsd_KCB9dbJ8WXE_DrLc5E5-4pnRYCQhyHJJ-IrjWYwpGens3VKw/4t3/I8Rvxi4FSDyuGeSaF3WM-g/h30/h001.ufyGOYgPPqykKadV2GndXMR7RdGBVK_zskIjnMjfT7M">unsubscribe</a></div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">CareerGrowth</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.yC9eJs5Q3flddlsql05gYaZDMkZAxi6vQVONBN2-QpKs66DZcvCpefyblSRLQfUOolJJ7xEP1ELITrdTZzgamXJyiVFYysA-rrMAelOxVrx5uyTV4zwNCM1GZhK7O01gW_H5GyhQRktTcE3t40XePwCYuEHFtyPqEkm0AZ_xBjhXKtt6UlmfYcjrChDVVIwU/4t3/I8Rvxi4FSDyuGeSaF3WM-g/h14/h001.6RsdRSUtstqH274SRgnSb2KRwDv1_F2LVf2pI9OXGmU">Growing As An Engineer In A World Of AI</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="growing-as-an-engineer-in-a-world-of-ai-ef3faf"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">There&#39;s a lot of buzz that AI means companies only want seniors now. That there&#39;s no room left for early career engineers. I don&#39;t think that&#39;s true. The thing actually worth worrying about isn&#39;t whether you&#39;ll get hired, it&#39;s whether you keep learning in a world of AI, now that it removes the friction where that learning used to happen. And that, thankfully, is a choice you get to make.</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Pointer &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.PFjHD-ii0vvUj1FcNnOS5qqF3eJEkrxympSzSDDZUB29F4951mhY03iCawzSQ6poZedL3n7H-lMzRTsKnY9bvtDfJYA8pICpvyyNYWT98Xtr9WdPcSpxxaa7Q5dr87aiYGihRTwZYkR0HjoovPrejageBVLzHH9tG8n84m3dFIuMnCMDviQfRQLGnrFY4wdh3h9Zwffqoap7IahStn1Zx9FxBC0dbqtZcIWs5Qb2lN3W7NPzwNLQCfjXO_oDddTMOMO_mDbiK4VXjp1fg0-PLD5T9qz5DNPZ4gPMhln17AcwX5HCUm5nV8yifu3QO6FA3jpiAkxgp2Mga6WTGfDYbfAq_T7sK9zfaJAm00tC2QunGj2lY86lBE1dFZp5U6N_im05m5AIB2IAgDvWKJ3YhaB56b4WVanjznLY5Bvo_orK3zGNNsqe9Fz-h354g125n0GK31bnyYRICxTARq7LIQcIbxkh2-GC2sY0FTySAcgNi5v83clGhv8d5LZFgU6MZAxYta1vlgcYL0Y25YRfZ3yYWPPDI-LWTVEBF2_6tzVavgtSBxtb7oLFpmuv_cQ0c9fh6G6tostqN1n6B_0dRwcLo59JmH6XsbaIW-96oRi4PTJpCTy5avyoqVYEgUPMgUD9IoCGpOrlGLE8xP5OmnrjFk-gxKhBberFaQhbUA_3gZN0jQBfqMwHzbKhl0Prhr8kw71jWOxARr4om1Dh67yfV6bUA3OTNRS6Q-1cAWi9DGrOKMhpYg-sRiHIeOpfMmJULlis7grAKzRUCNZL9hDIrWT67t46do1V3Y0Y9fpuV_5fbXsSJtWcl3JdKIVEa_6bhhpDeVb3q_yuzXYEsCKii1OeVAiNU9Tsd_KCB9dbJ8WXE_DrLc5E5-4pnRYCQhyHJJ-IrjWYwpGens3VKw/4t3/I8Rvxi4FSDyuGeSaF3WM-g/h30/h001.ufyGOYgPPqykKadV2GndXMR7RdGBVK_zskIjnMjfT7M">unsubscribe</a></div>

<h2 class="issue-category-label">Databases</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid issue-story-grid-single">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.J3WaaJcD-G_P1A1xsf5CHpaRz8t_03O-U4BCRI-69w3vDn_EFLOpWPHdtPi1CfDNy5uxWc8oAZhCkZiLMem5gPZg4XrbR_taCi46aXk-Hjn03XKbJoP9YzUs12cE9oYh6e7ZRuI7ABaeT0tA884aOYt0pEhmLUe3Xkre1lTDlNZfaf79lup8k7rY94zYDOzsNNEm-3q8FX7Fe4KxAzlqaW8nEa0Km78cQaowi0lJ2u4/4t3/I8Rvxi4FSDyuGeSaF3WM-g/h16/h001.rfypncEN2g3SPuKs8CWvIfgxeNfF8y9O50vmjFrzpfE">Beyond Happy Path Engineering: Databases</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="beyond-happy-path-engineering-databases-c0e4f1"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">This series is about making your programs more resilient. Each post looks at one part of the system we take for granted: networks, time, databases, storage, users, dependencies, deployments, queues, and concurrency. The question is always the same: what assumptions are we making here, and what should we do when they stop being true?</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">Pointer &middot; via aiste.ulozaite@gmail.com &middot; <a href="https://link.mail.beehiiv.com/ss/c/u001.PFjHD-ii0vvUj1FcNnOS5qqF3eJEkrxympSzSDDZUB29F4951mhY03iCawzSQ6poZedL3n7H-lMzRTsKnY9bvtDfJYA8pICpvyyNYWT98Xtr9WdPcSpxxaa7Q5dr87aiYGihRTwZYkR0HjoovPrejageBVLzHH9tG8n84m3dFIuMnCMDviQfRQLGnrFY4wdh3h9Zwffqoap7IahStn1Zx9FxBC0dbqtZcIWs5Qb2lN3W7NPzwNLQCfjXO_oDddTMOMO_mDbiK4VXjp1fg0-PLD5T9qz5DNPZ4gPMhln17AcwX5HCUm5nV8yifu3QO6FA3jpiAkxgp2Mga6WTGfDYbfAq_T7sK9zfaJAm00tC2QunGj2lY86lBE1dFZp5U6N_im05m5AIB2IAgDvWKJ3YhaB56b4WVanjznLY5Bvo_orK3zGNNsqe9Fz-h354g125n0GK31bnyYRICxTARq7LIQcIbxkh2-GC2sY0FTySAcgNi5v83clGhv8d5LZFgU6MZAxYta1vlgcYL0Y25YRfZ3yYWPPDI-LWTVEBF2_6tzVavgtSBxtb7oLFpmuv_cQ0c9fh6G6tostqN1n6B_0dRwcLo59JmH6XsbaIW-96oRi4PTJpCTy5avyoqVYEgUPMgUD9IoCGpOrlGLE8xP5OmnrjFk-gxKhBberFaQhbUA_3gZN0jQBfqMwHzbKhl0Prhr8kw71jWOxARr4om1Dh67yfV6bUA3OTNRS6Q-1cAWi9DGrOKMhpYg-sRiHIeOpfMmJULlis7grAKzRUCNZL9hDIrWT67t46do1V3Y0Y9fpuV_5fbXsSJtWcl3JdKIVEa_6bhhpDeVb3q_yuzXYEsCKii1OeVAiNU9Tsd_KCB9dbJ8WXE_DrLc5E5-4pnRYCQhyHJJ-IrjWYwpGens3VKw/4t3/I8Rvxi4FSDyuGeSaF3WM-g/h30/h001.ufyGOYgPPqykKadV2GndXMR7RdGBVK_zskIjnMjfT7M">unsubscribe</a></div>

<div class="issue-category-body">
<h2 class="issue-category-label">Peopleware</h2>
<div class="issue-story-grid">
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="https://us.list-manage.com/GXkvdNYYsct?e=86169b0ba2&amp;c2id=4541cead0ded4f764194eec0f1f5b20f">Being a Staff Engineer at Stripe in 2026</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="being-a-staff-engineer-at-stripe-in-2026-07ab44"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">A lot of it is generic and does not provide practical insight to use, still, one thing that is important to get comfortable (and learn to find balance) with is this: &quot;There is a genuine pressure to get things done quickly, because there are always 10 more things in the pipeline waiting to get done.&quot;</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://softwareleadweekly.us6.list-manage.com/unsubscribe?u=1a258e0fefbb23214c59c5a8d&amp;id=efe3d3cd5b&amp;t=b&amp;e=86169b0ba2&amp;c=22ba65c4f8">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/using-agents-is-literally-engineering-management-b9430d/">Using Agents Is Literally Engineering Management</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="using-agents-is-literally-engineering-management-b9430d"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">Wonderful post by Jack Danger with a great take that truly captures the early days in managing humans and AI: &quot;A first-time manager cuts hard toward either micromanaging or abdication... That&#39;s the IC-and-AI spectrum right now. Either you hand-hold every line of output, or you YOLO it to production and hope. [...] let yourself accept that all of the models and all of the people are imperfect. Then commit to building the skills.&quot;</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://softwareleadweekly.us6.list-manage.com/unsubscribe?u=1a258e0fefbb23214c59c5a8d&amp;id=efe3d3cd5b&amp;t=b&amp;e=86169b0ba2&amp;c=22ba65c4f8">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
<div class="issue-story">
<h3 class="issue-story-heading"><a href="/2026/08/16/stories/a-fresh-definition-of-the-product-role-7bfa42/">A Fresh Definition of the Product Role</a> <span class="reads-controls" data-reads-slug="a-fresh-definition-of-the-product-role-7bfa42"></span></h3>
<div class="issue-story-blurb">If the cost of building is lower, the value of knowing what to build is much higher. The problem is that many product managers think they&#39;re great because they can articulate what to build. Great PMs can deeply articulate why to build: &quot;the hard part is knowing that it should exist, and knowing how it should exist.&quot;</div>
<div class="issue-story-meta">uloza+hey@proton.me &middot; <a href="https://softwareleadweekly.us6.list-manage.com/unsubscribe?u=1a258e0fefbb23214c59c5a8d&amp;id=efe3d3cd5b&amp;t=b&amp;e=86169b0ba2&amp;c=22ba65c4f8">unsubscribe</a></div>
</div>
</div>
</div>
</div>
{% endraw %}
